市場疑慮拖累美股，短線聚焦 Fed 決策與財報
2026 年 9 月 15 日
一、 9/14 美股下挫、美債殖利率曲線熊市趨平，且油價大漲
1. 股票指數：
⚫ 道瓊工業指數下跌 0.29%，收 52,421.20 點；
⚫ S&P 500 指數下挫 0.48%，收 7,619.98 點；
⚫ 那斯達克綜合指數下跌 0.56%，收 26,186.41 點；
⚫ 費城半導體指數下跌 5.86%，收 11,131.28 點。
2. 當日重要個股表現：
⚫ 在科技類股方面，輝達下跌約 3.4%、美光下跌 5.3%、邁威爾下跌 7.3%；

⚫ 通訊類股方面，Netflix 上漲 3.8%、Take-Two 上漲 3.5%、Fox 上漲 4.6%；
⚫ 軟體類股方面 ，ServiceNow、Adobe、Workday 等軟體股則上漲約 4% 至
7.4%。
3. 美國債市漲跌不一、殖利率曲線走勢熊市趨平。2 年期美國公債殖利率上升 3.24 個基
點至 4.66%，10 年期殖利率上升 2.06 個基點至 4.99%，10 年期殖利率一度升破 5%，
而 30 年期殖利率則下跌約 0.76 個基點至 5.34%。短率上升，反映市場對未來美國聯
準會升息的預期。
4. 油價因中東衝突與供給風險再度升高而上漲 ，WTI 原油上漲 1.81%，收在每桶 101.86
美元，布蘭特原油則上漲 1.56%，收在 106.24 美元。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. AI 開發放緩疑慮，壓抑硬體需求預期：Anthropic 執行長 Dario Amodei 於上週末
呼籲放慢前沿 AI 能力提升速度，並提出第三方安全評估與業界協調方案，使 AI 擴

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
張節奏成為新的不確定因素。我們研判，半導體位於 AI 建置支出的上游，股價對新
增算力投資的成長速度尤其敏感。即使既有訂單未立即改變，只要市場降低對未來數
季增速的期待，GPU、高頻寬記憶體及網通設備供應鏈的評價便可能先行調整；目前
仍須區分投資預期降溫與實際訂單下修。
2. 中東能源設施遇襲，油價推升通膨風險：沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施及中東船舶遇襲，引
發供應疑慮，布蘭特原油期貨 9 月 14 日結算價升至每桶 106.24 美元。油價持續偏
高可能透過運輸與生產成本以及民眾實質購買力影響企業獲利；若進一步傳導至通膨

預期，將延長貨幣政策維持緊縮的時間，對股市評價形成壓力。
3. 十年美債殖利率一度突破 5%，評價與融資承壓：美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中突破
5%，為 2023 年以來首見。殖利率上升提高未來獲利的折現率，對依賴長期成長預
期支撐評價的股票較不利。另一方面，資料中心若仰賴借款擴張，融資成本上升也會
提高投資報酬門檻，可能影響新增建置計畫，形成 AI 供應鏈面臨的另一項壓力。
4. 通膨月增回升 ， 升息前觀望氣氛加重 ：BLS 於 9 月 11 日公布 ，8 月 CPI 月增 0.4%
，高於 7 月的 0.1%，核心 CPI 月增率也由 0.2% 升至 0.3%。我們認為，短期物
價動能回升，加上能源價格偏高，提高聯準會維持緊縮政策的壓力。投資人除了評估
當次升息，也須重新衡量後續利率高點與維持時間，增加 FOMC 會議前高波動股票
的調整壓力。
5. 成長與評價雙重重估，放大半導體跌幅：本波股市回檔壓力同時來自獲利成長預期與
市場願給的評價倍數。AI 開發放緩疑慮影響未來營收想像，殖利率上行則壓低未來獲

利的現值。當股價已反映較高成長期待，只要新增訊息不如預期，即使當期營收仍強
勁，也可能出現明顯修正。後續應觀察獲利上修能否抵銷評價收縮，不能僅以跌幅大
就認定股價便宜。

不另行通知。
三、市場看法
1. 龍頭財報仍具成長，支持中期正向看法：輝達 8 月 26 日公布的 2027 會計年度第
二季資料中心營收達 890 億美元、年增 117%，並預估第三季總營收 1,080 億美元
、上下浮動 2%。博通 9 月 2 日公布的 2026 會計年度第三季 AI 半導體營收為
167 億美元、年增 221%，並預估第四季達 217 億美元。上述實績與公司展望，是
我們對 AI 核心供應鏈維持中期正向看法的依據；惟這些財測發布於本次事件之前，
仍須追蹤後續更新。
2. 放緩方案尚待落地，需求影響須分項評估：Anthropic 執行長 Amodei 明確表示，放

緩不代表停止模型訓練，但也提出研議訓練算力等限制，因此實際影響取決於後續執
行方式。既有模型的商業使用、推論與安全測試仍可能帶來算力需求，惟能否抵銷前
沿訓練投資的放緩，必須由使用量、訂單與收入驗證。此時適合保留成長觀點，同時
提高對支出變化的敏感度。
3. 科技股仍有輪動，軟體與半導體強弱分明：9/14 當日企業軟體與資安股逆勢上漲，顯
示科技股內部仍有輪動與受惠程度的差異。我們認為，若前沿 AI 開發放緩，部分既
有軟體業者面臨的競爭及產品替代壓力可能減輕；AI 安全疑慮升高，也可能強化資安
、權限控管及企業治理需求。相較之下，半導體更直接承受新增建置支出預期變化。
因此，9/14 當日費半重挫尚不足以推論整體科技需求同步轉弱；後續宜比較訂單能見
度、訂閱收入、獲利與現金流，單日相對強勢仍須由基本面延續性驗證。
4. 升息機率約九成，會後政策態度才是關鍵：依 Reuters 於美東 9 月 14 日收盤報導
引述的 CME FedWatch 資料，本次升息一碼的機率約 90%。若美東 9 月 16 日

FOMC 確實升息 0.25 個百分點，利率區間將升至 3.75% 至 4.00%，當次升息已
大致反映於市場預期。我們認為，會後聲明及主席記者會對未來利率政策的態度才是
關鍵：是否暗示還需升息、利率須維持高檔多久，以及是否擔心能源漲價向核心通膨

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
擴散，並須搭配點陣圖判讀。若後續緊縮訊號未超出預期、長債殖利率趨穩，有利評
價修復；若利率路徑較預期更高、更持久，科技股仍可能承壓。
5. 通膨改善仍不均衡，避免過早判定轉折：8 月核心 CPI 年增率為 2.4%，低於 7 月
的 2.5%，但其月增率由 0.2% 升至 0.3%，顯示年增放緩與短期動能回升並存。後
續須觀察能源漲價是否向服務價格與通膨預期擴散。若油價回穩、核心物價再度降溫
，利率壓力可望緩和；若能源衝擊持續，股市調整時間可能拉長。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦 Fed 決策與 10 月美股財報，科技股中期向上趨勢料不致有變：我們目前仍

維持 AI 核心供應鏈中期正向看法，較看好能將營收成長轉化為獲利與現金流的企業
，但宜密切觀察本週的 FOMC 會議結果與 10 月中將起跑的美股財報。若雲端業者維
持資本支出、晶片公司財測穩定，且 FOMC 此次會議未釋出超預期緊縮訊號，有利
市場信心修復；然而，若出現廣泛支出下修、訂單遞延，或油價與殖利率持續上行，
則應拉長觀察期並重新檢視通膨與利率對於股市動能前景的影響。
2. 3~10 年美國公債/投資級債為債市首選：目前美國的通膨數據仍具韌性，且就業市場
穩健，使年底前市場升息預期再度升溫。市場對美國財政赤字、公債供給及長期通膨
仍要求較高風險補償，30 年期殖利率與短天期利差仍維持高檔，顯示期限溢酬尚未收
斂，考量當前 3～10 年期美國公債與投資級債能鎖定目前較高息收，且其受長端財政
風險影響也相對較小，故現階段3～10 年期美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對
較佳。

市場焦點
聯準會9月
升息一碼
• 聯邦基準利率升至 3.75%~4.00%，經濟預測部分上調2026-2028年GDP成長率、失業率皆下
調，通膨降溫進程仍相對緩慢 ，點陣圖顯示今年還有升息一碼的機會 ，這次升息對股票而言 ，
聯準會沒有準備以大幅犧牲經濟與就業換取通膨下降 ，降低了硬著陸風險；對債券而言 ，短
端仍須消化更高的政策利率，但長端反而可能受惠於抗通膨可信度改善。
AI模型
開發放緩
• Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度。不過，前沿模型開發速度放
緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。晶片、算力、資料中心及能源等AI基礎建設仍處於供
不應求格局 ，短期若因市場情緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔 ，較可能反映估值與持倉修正 ，

而非AI需求基本面反轉 。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化 ，且全球總體經濟及地緣政治
風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。
台灣央行
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融
通利率分別維持2%、2.375%和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制 ，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最
高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通膨率 ，GDP上修至11.48%、CPI
及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16。(4)央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表
現，不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。

貨幣決議與9月、7月聲明稿差異，不再強調能源衝擊 全員支持升息
• 聯準會9月升息一碼，聯邦基準利率在3.75%~4.00%。
聲明稿重點差異
紅色字體為9月的新增部分，括號內藍色字體為刪除的7月聲明措辭
FOMC以12票贊成升息一碼 批准發布以下聲明 ：委員會決定 ，將聯邦基金利率目標 區間上調至 3.75%至4%維持在
3.50%至3.75%，以支持聯準會的雙重使命。委員會延續其維持銀行體系充足準備金的政策。
儘管中東衝突在一定程度上帶來了高水準的不確定性 ，美國經濟活動持續以穩健步調擴張 。儘管地緣政治情勢 等因素
使經濟前景仍籠 罩高度不確定性，但國內支出保持韌性，生產力成長強勁，企業資本投資也相當強勁穩健。新增就業

人數大致跟上勞動力成長速度，失業率也變化不大，顯示就業市場目前仍大致維持穩定。
相較於委員會2%的目標，通貨膨脹率依然偏高 ，這在一定程度上反映了部分特定部門 (包括能源)因供給衝擊所導致的
價格上漲。今日的政策行動，將有助於讓通膨更及時地回到2%目標，委員會將實現價格穩定。

聯準會會後記者會問答重點整理
1
通膨「過高且持續太久」 當
前重心偏向物價穩定
通膨數據未改善，通膨過高且持續太久，聯準會當前的政策重心偏向雙重使命中的物價穩定 。透過政策
行動表明，聯準會對抑制通膨是認真的。
2
經濟、就業與地緣政治促成
一致升息
近幾週公布的數據證實美國經濟正在走強 ，基礎經濟成長率高於先前判斷；其次 ，勞動市場依然穩健；
第三，通膨持續高於目標，地緣政治局勢也改變經濟展望 。華許表示，這三項因素共同促成當天「堅定
且一致」的決定。
3
點狀圖暗示今年再升 1碼 但
不是政策承諾
最新經濟預測顯示，多數官員認為今年仍有進一步升息空間。12 名官員預期年底前再升息 1 碼，另有 4
人預期還會升息 2 碼，僅 2 人主張利率維持在目前水準。不過，點狀圖反映的是官員對適當利率路徑的

個別預測，並非正式的承諾。
4
拒絕提供前瞻指引 不預判下
次會議結果
面對下一次升息可能落在 10 月或 12 月的追問，華許表示「我不給出前瞻指引。」
他強調，先前公開表達的是對貨幣政策紀律的承諾 ，而不是對特定利率決策的承諾 。聯準會不會預判未
來任何會議結果，後續行動仍將依據最新經濟數據及展望作出決定。
5
升息非市場逼迫 迴避川普施
壓問題
面對此次升息是否由債市波動及市場壓力所推動的提問，華許予以否認。
他表示是根據就業走勢、經濟強度及整體情勢作出決定 ，市場價格可作為觀察依據，但不能決定聯準會
的政策。
資料來源：Fed，華爾街見聞，2026.9.17

台灣利率連10凍 明年經濟展望佳
• 利率決議重點： (1)台灣央行宣布基準利率維持不變 ，重貼現率、擔保放款融通利率和短期融通利率分別維持 2%、2.375%
和4.25%。(2)放鬆房市信用管制，調整自然人第2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限，由6成調升為7成。(3)上修今年經濟成長率及通
膨率，GDP上修至11.48%(六月預估9.45 %)、CPI及核心CPI分別上修至2.03%及2.16%(六月預估1.91%及1.90%)。
• 觀點：全球AI資本支出維持上調的趨勢，台股供應鏈訂單直接受惠，央行預估明年AI基礎建設需求將持續貢獻台灣出口表現，
不過由於基期已墊高，預測明年台灣經濟成長率為5.82%。
• 台股短線展望：上週隨著聯準會利率決議公布後，台股量能回升，顯示短線消息面或已利空出盡。權值股台積電領軍台灣加

權指數站上月線，技術指標偏正向。投資佈局方面，由於地緣政治、通膨及貿易關稅風險仍存在不確定性，建議AI概念持股
核心布局行業龍頭企業，留意中小型成長股較易受到外部因素而股價大幅波動。
資料來源：中央銀行，2026.9.17。f代表央行預測數。

美國零售強勁回升擴大內需支撐，惟價格壓力與升息提高市場波動
• 零售全面回升，消費動能展現廣度：美國8月零售銷售月增1.2%，創3月以來最大增幅，優於市場預期，並扭轉7月修正
後月減0.5%；非實體店面銷售月增 2.6%、電子及家電增 1.6%，餐飲服務亦增 1.2%，且扣除汽車與汽油後仍增 1.2%，
控制組更增1.4%，顯示消費回升並非僅由汽車與燃料支出帶動，第三季內需仍具支撐。
• 名目消費偏強，通膨與升息增添需求壓力： 不過8月零售銷售年增6.0%屬名目增幅，部分反映物價上升因素；同期 CPI
年增3.4%，汽油價格月增3.9%，能源成本上升可能壓縮家庭實質購買力 ，加上Fed於9月決議升息1碼，後續利率政策
仍將視通膨與經濟數據調整，使市場焦點由消費回升轉向評估高利率與高物價對後續需求動能的影響。

• 後市觀察：短期零售回升仍有助支撐美國景氣，但若能源成本持續走高，加上升息後借貸成本上升，消費動能能否維持
將成為重要觀察點；投資配置可偏向美國投資級短中天期債券 ，兼顧收益與利率風險，股票則可聚焦獲利穩定、具定價
能力的大型優質企業，降低需求放緩與資金成本升高的影響。

顧 • AI安全監管升溫，短期壓抑市場情緒：Anthropic、OpenAI及xAI相繼支持放緩最先進AI模型開發速度，並引入獨立第三方
評估等安全機制，引發市場對AI資本支出降溫的疑慮。不過，前沿模型開發速度放緩，並不代表AI基礎建設需求同步轉弱。
Alphabet、微軟及亞馬遜近期AI相關積壓訂單持續攀升，合計達約1.64兆美元，其中微軟及Alphabet分別突破6,000億及
5,000億美元，搭配雲端業務持續成長，顯示企業AI需求仍具韌性，相關訂單有望逐步轉化為未來數年的營收與獲利。
• 槓桿壓力逐步消化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值：美國雲端巨頭擴大AI資本支出，壓抑短期自由現金流；相較之下，亞洲
科技企業自由現金流可望由目前約2,450億增至2028年的6,330億美元，支撐亞洲科技供應鏈中長期發展。短期若因市場情

緒或資金槓桿調整而出現回檔，較可能反映估值與持倉修正，而非AI需求基本面反轉。後續若前期累積的槓桿壓力逐步消化，
且全球總體經濟及地緣政治風險未進一步惡化，AI供應鏈仍具中長期配置價值。

油價在美國中期選舉前，或將維持在高檔
資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.14
伊朗戰爭期間卡達油氣收入暴跌
卡達政府的石油和天然氣收入 美伊衝突
• 美伊衝突短期內仍難見明確解方，彭博預估雙方後續可能採取的行動，主要可分為以下四種情境：
(1)伊朗承受經濟封鎖：伊朗承受美國經濟制裁與施壓，避免進一步重大軍事升級。在美方護航行動支持下，商船仍可維持通行
荷姆茲海峽，國際原油供應未出現明顯中斷，油價預估在每桶80美元區間波動。
(2)伊朗將攻擊範圍限於海灣地區油輪與美軍：伊朗主要鎖定海灣地區能源運輸船隻及美軍目標，美方則採取有限度軍事回應，

衝突維持在一定範圍內，油價預估在每桶90美元區間波動。
(3)伊朗擴大攻擊範圍：攻擊目標不再局限於海灣地區船隻及美軍基地，海灣能源基礎設施以及紅海等重要航運咽喉，均可能成
為目標。局勢升級期間，油價可能維持在每桶100美元以上，待衝突緩和後回落至70至80美元區間，此情境發生的可能性最高。
(4)美伊雙方全面擴大開戰：伊朗進一步擴大攻擊目標，導致美國軍事資產及區域能源基礎設施遭受重大破壞，甚至不排除以色
列重新加入戰局，油價可能升至每桶120美元。

全球債市拋售勢頭不墜，AI融資競逐資金推升美債殖利率
• 長端殖利率走高，財政與通膨壓力仍是主因 ：近期美國長
天期公債殖利率持續走高，其中10年期美債殖利率更突破
5%，創2007年以來高點；除能源價格上升推升通膨疑慮
外，美國財政赤字、政府債務及持續性的發債需求 ，也提
高市場對長天期公債供給與期限溢酬的要求 ，反映長端利
率壓力仍未完全紓解。
• AI企業擴大融資，增加公司債供給與資金競逐： AI資料中
心、晶片與電力基礎建設的資本支出快速增加 ，帶動大型
科技企業提高債券融資規模；截至 6月，華爾街市場策略
研究機構Yardeni Research在追蹤外國私人投資人過去一
年買進美國公司債約3,900億美元，高於美國公債約3,290
億美元，顯示資金配置確有轉向公司債的現象 ，不過市場

研究亦指出，AI企業發債對美債殖利率的直接推升效果相
對有限，應可視為推動長天期利率上行的眾多因素之一。
• 後市觀察：若財政融資與AI基礎建設支出持續維持高檔 ，
長天期公債仍面臨較大的供給與資金配置壓力；但 AI投資
仍具中長期成長動能 ，配置上可關注資料中心 、半導體及
電力基礎建設供應鏈 ，固定收益則以短中天期美債及高品
質投資級公司債為主，降低長天期利率波動的影響。

新興市場本幣債券擁高票息提供防禦力
• 高票息提供防禦力：對債券投資人而言，票息就像安全墊。當市場利率上升、債券價格下跌時，持續收到的票息可以部分甚
至完全抵銷價格損失。新興市場本幣政府債券今年的表現，比市場原先預期更具韌性。目前新興市場本幣債券的持有收益約
為4.54%，已經足以對利率風險形成相當程度的保護 ，即使今年存續期間對多數市場造成負面影響 ，票息收益仍讓整體報酬
維持正數。這也是為什麼新興市場本幣債券在美伊衝突爆發後一度重挫超過 6%，但之後仍能逐步反彈 。哥倫比亞、巴西、
匈牙利及墨西哥的債券表現尤其突出。
• 市場已反映相當程度的升息風險：問題在於未來幾個月投資環境可能比上半年更加困難，能源價格上漲推升通膨，也迫使新
興市場央行維持較為鷹派的貨幣政策 。市場目前甚至預期 ，19個主要新興市場中 ，有18個市場的債券殖利率可能在年底前

上升。殖利率上升意味著債券價格承壓，因此投資人不能只看表面的高票息，必須同時評估未來利率變化可能造成資本損失。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026.9.15

新興市場本幣債券目前最大風險在於利率與匯率
• 存續期間仍是今年最主要的拖累因素：一般而言，債券的存續期間越高，當殖利率上升時，債券價格下跌的幅度就可能越大。
今年19個符合基準指數條件的新興市場中 ，有14個市場的總報酬受到存續期間拖累 ，其中韓國、馬來西亞、印尼、泰國及
菲律賓受到的負面影響最為明顯。更值得注意的是，新興市場債券的利率表現與美國公債高度相關，因此，投資新興市場債
券，不能只觀察當地央行，也必須密切留意美國聯準會利率政策及美國公債殖利率走勢。
• 匯率可能成為下一個重要變數：新興市場本幣債券除了債券價格本身，還存在另一項重要風險：匯率。今年即期匯率變動反
而對新興市場本幣債券帶來正面貢獻 ，約增加57個基點。但如果能源價格持續上漲並形成滯脹環境 ，順周期貨幣可能面臨

更大的貶值壓力 。目前市場預期年底前新興市場貨幣整體仍偏弱 ，19種新興市場貨幣中 ，有11種預計兌美元貶值 。其中土
耳其、哥倫比亞及巴西貨幣的潛在跌幅相對較大。這代表即使債券本身提供不錯的票息，如果當地貨幣大幅貶值，換算成美
元或其他投資人本國貨幣後，仍可能侵蝕甚至吞噬債券收益。
• 小結與投資建議：目前新興市場本幣債券比較像是高票息換取較高波動的投資，而不是單純追求資本利得的交易。在殖利率
已處於相對高檔、但利率與匯率風險仍未完全解除的情況下，採取分散市場、控制部位、逢回布局的策略會比追高更為合適。

• 多重資產配置可有效風險管理：美伊衝突持續及聯準會利率政策，使今年金融市場震盪加劇，加上第四季美國期中選舉到來，
增加市場更多不確定性，不過從歷史經驗來看，地緣政治事件對股市的影響多屬短期現象，市場在初期情緒性下跌後，通常會
逐步回歸基本面，相較於預測事件發展，投資人更應透過多元資產配置分散風險，以提升投資組合的穩定性與長期抗震能力。
• 透過多重資產廣納收益來源：在利率維持相對高檔的環境下，投資人更應重視收益來源的多元化，從下圖可見，相較於過去幾
年，掩護性買權、非投資等級債，及投資等級債等資產的收益率均顯著提升，提供投資組合更具吸引力的現金流來源，其中掩
護性買權收益率已超過10%，非投資等級債，及投資等級債亦分別達7%及5%以上水準，透過結合不同收益型資產，不僅有助

提升整體收益潛力，更能降低對單一市場或利率變動的依賴，在市場波動加劇時提高投資組合的穩定度與韌性，兼顧收益與長
期成長目標。

前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

Company Description 
TSMC was founded in 1987 and is listed in Taiwan 
(2330TT) and the US (TSM/NYS).TSMC is the largest and 
global leader in integrated circuit (IC) manufacturing. As a 
build-to-order foundry, it provides a wide range of value-
add activities: IC manufacturing, mask-making, IC design 
services, turnkey solutions, and process development. We 
attribute its success to its proven, winning business model, 
unparalleled scale advantage, optimized execution, and 
technology scope and depth. 5 shares = 1 ADR.

Investment Rationale 
The ongoing semi content growth in mobile, rise of artificial 
intelligence (AI), and proliferation of Internet of Things (IoT) 
should result in sustainable upside in aggregate computing 
power globally. TSMC, as the leading contract manufacturer 
of semiconductor chips, is in a good position to capitalize. 
We are structurally positive on TSMC and expect it to 
sustain over 15% growth with rising FCF from 2026 
onwards.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co. (TSMWF / TSM) 
Our PO for TSMC is NT$3,260 per share (US$620 per ADR), based on 20x 2027E P/E. 
TSMC's valuation has traded in the range of 10-26x PE from 2017. 20x is in the mid to 
high end of the historical range, above the average P/E of 16x. We think a PE above the 
average is justified given TSMC's enhanced industry position and mid-to-high 50% 5-
year CAGR in AI should underpin a rerating

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) greater-than-expected slowdown in global 
smartphone/consumer electronics demand, (2) Intel's potential insourcing strategy and 
ambitions in foundry service, (3) execution risks on advanced nodes, and (4) higher than 
expected tariffs.

公司拜訪快報

晟田(4541)：航太及半導體客戶需求延續，帶動 2027 年獲利成長 
建議：中立轉買進

一、晟田為航太精密機械零件加工廠 
晟田成立於 1991 年 8 月，實收資本額 6.76 億元，截至
2Q26 底，員工約 686 位。主要從事航太零組件(包括發
動機機匣與懸塊、起落架)及機械零組件(包括半導體真
空泵浦零組件、食品飲料填充包裝設備零組件、工業自
動化精密傳動零組件等產品)之製造加工與銷售等業務。
2025 年前五大客戶為漢翔(2634)、日本 E 客戶、
SLS(Safran Landing Systems)、SPP (6355.JP)及長榮
航太(2645)。2026 年前 9 個月產品別營收佔比為航太零
組件(客供料) 48%，航太零組件(自購料) 25%，半導體
設備零組件 23%，其他 4%。

二 、 夏季電價及費用認列影響獲利 ， 預估3Q26 稅後 EPS 
1.26 元 
晟田 3Q26 營收 5.6 億元(+1.9%QoQ，+30.9%YoY)，
季增主因為受惠航太客供料客戶拉貨動能成長及半導體
客戶需求提升。獲利方面，儘管產品組合持續優化，惟
受到夏季電價及籌資相關費用認列影響，預估營業利益
1.01 億元(-6%QoQ，+97.1%YoY)。假設匯率持穩，預
估稅後淨利 0.85 億元(-1%QoQ，+34.4%YoY)， 稅後EPS 
1.26 元。 
三、產品組合持續優化，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 1.3 元 
展望 4Q26，半導體方面，考量客戶需求持續強勁，有
望帶動出貨動能；航太方面，波音(BA.US) 737 系列機
型月產能有望於 2026 年底自 42 架提升至 47 架，另，
根據空中巴士(EADSY.US)規劃，2026 年預計交付 870
架，截至 Sep-26 交付達成率約 6 成，預期 4Q26 將加
速交機 ， 帶動航太零組件拉貨需求 。 整體而言 ， 預估4Q26
營收 5.62 億元(+0.5%QoQ，+20.7%YoY)。獲利方面，
隨產品組合持續優化，且無電價因素干擾，預估營業利
益 1.05 億元(+3.9%QoQ，+28.6%YoY)。 假設匯率持穩，
預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 1.3 元。 
四、需求延續，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 4.51 元 
展望後市，儘管航太供應鏈仍受原物料短缺、人力及產
能瓶頸影響，惟波音及空巴在手訂單仍維持高位，顯示
終端需求具支撐。隨供應鏈瓶頸逐步緩解，預期將帶動
航太零組件拉貨需求回升，有利晟田營運成長。半導體
方面，受惠終端客戶持續擴大資本支出，帶動相關產品
需求穩定成長。整體而言，預估 2026 年營收 21.2 億元
(+29.1%YoY)。獲利方面，隨產品組合持續優化，預估
毛利率 25.1%，稅後淨利 3.1 億元(+122.9%YoY)，稅後
EPS 4.51 元。 
展望 2027 年，考量航空引擎零組件客戶需求強勁，晟
田規劃擴充相關產能，新廠有望於 2H27 投產；半導體
方面，持續優化相關產能，以因應客戶需求成長。整體
而言，預估 2027 年營收 23.5 億元(+10.7%YoY)。獲利
方面，考量新廠投產後折舊費用增加，預估毛利率年減
至 24.8%， 稅後淨利3.3 億元(+9%YoY)， 稅後EPS 4.58
元(以增資稀釋後股本計算)。 
五、中立轉買進理由 
評價方面，以 2027 年 EPS 估算，目前 PE 約為 13X，
介於晟田歷史區間 12X~20X 下緣 ， 調升至買進評等 ， 目
標價 75 元(2026F PE 17X)。

王文雯 wenwen.wang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：電話訪談

矽力*-KY (6415 TT) 
資料中心與車載為成長主軸

永豐觀點 
矽力杰 3Q26 營收優於預期，4Q26 展望正向，雖短期毛利率較為波動，不過隨資
料中心與車載訂單加持，以及第四代製程平滲透率提升， 應有助於中長期毛利率回
穩。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，2027
年稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營運拓展，研究部
維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

ESG 評析 
矽力*-KY 企業永續評鑑整體為 B 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面
向平均得分分別為 B+等與 B 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2023 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學)

專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。

### Page 2

2

矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠：矽力杰成立於 02/2008，為電源管理 IC 設計公司，
產品線包括 DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置
與 LED 背光驅動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。矽力杰 2Q26 產品終端應用主要區
分為四大領域，包括消費電子(如平板電腦、STB 與 TV)36%、工業應用(如 LED 照
明、伺服器與安防設備等)35%、資訊產品(如筆電)9%與網路通訊(如路由器、手機
等)6%、 車載14%。國際電源管理晶片設計同業與矽力杰產品類似者為 TI、Maxim、
ON Semi 與 ADI，台系類似的參與廠商則包括致新、茂達等。 
3Q26 營收優於預期 ，4Q26 展望正向 ：矽力杰總計 3Q26 營收 72.83 億元
(+21%QoQ，+53%YoY)，落於研究部原預估 3Q26 營收達成率 102%(研究部原
預估季增 18%)，優於預期，主要係由資料中心為最主要推升力道，再者車載因新
款晶片推出與市占率提升而增 、 消費電子即便市場需求較為疲弱， 但因公司新款晶
片推出，故反而仍能向上。目前展望 4Q26 亦維持正向看法，營收變化主要仍需視
後段封測產能的供給程度而定。研究部預估矽力杰 3Q26 毛利率 47.63%，稅後獲
利 11.32 億元(-28%QoQ，+66%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.9 元，並預估 4Q26 營收 76.9
億元(+5.5%QoQ，+43%YoY)，毛利率 47.8%，稅後獲利 12.33 億元(+9%QoQ，
+53%YoY)，稅後 EPS 3.13 元。 
主要產能瓶頸在後段封測 ：目前矽力杰最主要的產能瓶頸在後段封測的支應，主要
是後段封測供給被 AI 應用排擠，目前除原有封測廠的產能擴充外，公司策略也持
續驗證新封測廠，以其紓解封測產能緊缺問題。至於晶圓產能，則矽力杰因具備第
四代 12 吋製程平台產能(第四代製程平台滲透率由 2025 年的個位數比重 ， 已將提
升至 4Q26 的 20%)，故反而晶圓較能充足的支應，明年第五代製程平台亦將加入
量產範圍。針對 2026 年毛利率走勢仍較有變數，係因影響毛利率因子正負交錯：
正向因子包括公司依據產品與客戶別而有不等的漲價措施 、 第四代製程平台挹注與
產品結構變化，負向因子則包括晶圓/封測成本上升、存貨依據庫齡政策而有損失
或回沖認列等影響。不過研究部認為隨 07/01 已開始進行漲價，理應能消弭部分
製造成本攀升的衝擊，遂預估 2H26 毛利率應能優於 2Q26 水準，中長期隨第四
代製程平台滲透率提升與產品結構變化， 理應有助於毛利率趨勢向上。 相較毛利率
是否獲得顯著提升，目前公司更關心在希冀產能能獲得更充足的支援， 以滿足客戶
的需求。 
資料中心與車載為後續成長主軸：資料中心、車載與工業將成為推升後續成長的主
軸：(1) 資料中心(包括主板/企業級 SSD/光模組)目前營收比重 15%~16%，2027
年 目 前 維 持 預 期>20% 比 重 ， 公 司資 料 中 心 三 大 項 目 比 重 依 序 為光 通 訊
>SSD>MB，其中光通訊與 SSD 個別均為 mid-to-high single digit，MB 比重較
低。公司過往以 400G 以下為主，今年 400G/800G 均已導入，隨市占率逐步提
升，又未來若主板核心電源出貨，更將進一步推動資料中心業務規模擴大。公司亦
表示 PSU 相關產品出貨亦隨伺服器需求而增，公司 PSU 產品包括 AC-DC 
controller/ESD/Boost/ Buck/Temp sensor等，目前針對 VPD 進展還沒那麼快。
(2) 車載營收比重可望由去年的 13%， 攀升至今年的15%~20%， 研究部認為車載
動力來自於公司車載晶片供應品項增加， 每台車的可供應的內含價值擴大， 並且客
戶群攀升，除中國品牌上量外，也進一步拓展海外市場。(3) 儲能與光伏需求，以
及充電樁應用，亦能拓展工業領域的需求。 
維持買進： 研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年營收 258.35 億元(+37%YoY)，毛利率
47.65%，稅後獲利 47.05 億元(+90%YoY)，稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，
並預估 2027 年營收 341.9 億元(+32%YoY)，毛利率 48.44%，稅後獲利 60.39 億
元(+28%YoY)，稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營

營運現況與分析

矽光子、海外市場帶動營收持續穩健成長 
 維持買進，目標價 750 元：投顧認為隨汎銓竹北的 SAC-TEM 中心
開始貢獻先進製程 MA 收入，加以 AI 晶片及矽光子檢測需求顯著升
溫加持下，汎銓 2026 年營收成長近三成，毛利率提升至 30%以上。
此外，2027 年矽光子分析設備銷售將開始貢獻營收，估計 2026 年
EPS 4.34 元，2027 年 EPS 15.28 元，維持買進，目標價 750 元。 
 預期 3Q26 毛利率大幅優於前期 ，EPS 1.81 元：汎銓 3Q26 營收 7.59
億元 ，+19.6%QOQ，+31.6%YOY， 投顧估計毛利率將大幅優於2Q，
3Q26 EPS 1.81 元。 
 AI、矽光子、海外市場、矽光子分析設備等多動能齊發，預期 2027
年營運明顯走升：汎銓今年每月營運成本大致穩定在約 2.1 億元內，
27 年每月營運成本約 2.16 億元，只要營收持續成長，獲利將迅速放
大。為因應 AI 需求，AI 專區第三期擴產於 7 月完成並開始貢獻營
收，且計畫在年底前開始第四期擴產，對下半年業務樂觀看待，尤其
海外業績成長極為迅速 ， 預計到今年底 ， 海外業務佔比將有望達將近
40%。另外，汎銓已成功自主研發並組裝完成 3 台矽光子分析設備，
其核心光損偵測裝置技術已順利取得台灣、日本、美國及韓國專利。
標準版設備 9 月開放客戶驗證，11/5 正式對外實機展示，今年底開
始銷售，預期今年首批有 2~3 台設備可出貨，明年目標出貨 10 台，
標準機台售價約 6,000 多萬元 ， 此設備毛利率預期在50%以上水準，
若額外選配各項高階測試模組，單台售價可提升至 1 億至 3 億元。

個股報告

汎銓 主力為 MA （材料分
析 ） ， 營 收 佔 比超 八 成  
 汎銓成立於 2005 年 7 月，為檢測分析服務公司，協助客戶解決先進製程中遇到
的研發與品質問題，以提高客戶的生產效率，尤其在 AI 晶片、先進封裝、矽光
子領域有深厚布局 ，此外由於材料分析檢測交期平均在三天內、 故障分析檢測交
期在一周內，成為其競爭優勢。以營收比重來分，材料分析（MA）佔 2026 前 7
月營收比重 84％，IC 故障分析（FA）營收佔比 8％、矽光子+AI 晶片營收佔比
8％。 
汎銓有九個 MA 實驗室，三個 FA 實驗室，一個 RA 實驗室。2Q26 台灣營收佔
比 65%， 美國 、 日本 、 大陸等海外營收佔比35%， 目前全球員工人數共869 人。

Sources : Company、MasterLink

預期 3Q26 毛利率 大幅 優於 前
期，EPS 1.81 元 
 汎銓 2Q26 營收 6.35 億元，+9.8%QOQ，+16.6%YOY，隨 AI 晶片檢測分析等
高毛利業務需求強勁，毛利率回升至 24.96％，季增 3.4 個百分點，費用率降至
22.4%， 稅後EPS 0.35 元 ， 營業利益金額 1611 萬元 ， 稅後淨利金額 1860 萬元。
汎銓 3Q 營收 7.59 億元，+19.6%QOQ，+31.6%YOY，投顧估計毛利率將大幅
優於 2Q，3Q26 EPS 1.81 元。

AI 、 矽 光 子 、 海 外 市 場 、矽
光 子 分 析 設 備 等 多動能 齊
發 ， 預期 20 2 7 年 營運 明顯
走升  
 汎銓表示今年每月營運成本大致穩定在約 2.1 億元內，27 年每月營運成本約
2.16 億元，只要營收持續成長，獲利將迅速放大。今年 3 月起各個新建廠區已
開始貢獻業績，公司運營已從 3 月開始轉虧為盈。為因應 AI 需求，AI 專區第
三期擴產於 7 月完成並開始貢獻營收，且計畫在年底前開始第四期擴產 ，對下
半年業務樂觀看待，尤其海外業績成長極為迅速，預計到今年底，海外業務佔
比將有望達將近 40%。投顧預期汎銓 4Q26 營收季增 8.6%，年增 39.2%，估
計 4Q26 稅後 EPS 2.8 元。 
另外，汎銓已成功自主研發並組裝完成 3 台矽光子分析設備，其功能即是協助
客戶進行光損及漏光定位，並開發從晶片背面進行收光偵測的技術，鎖定矽光
子常用的 1310nm、1550nm 波段，協助定位 PIC 元件、光耦合及封裝過程中
的異常位置，且其核心光損偵測裝置技術已順利取得台灣、日本、美國及韓國
專利。對汎銓更具戰略意義的是，將不再侷限於檢測分析服務供應商的角色，
而是應客戶強烈需求，規劃進一步推出適用於量產端與品質驗證使用的矽光子
測試設備。標準版設備 9 月開放客戶驗證，11/5 正式對外實機展示，今年底開
始銷售，預期今年首批有 2~3 台設備可出貨，明年目標出貨 10 台，標準機台
售價約 6,000 多萬元，此設備毛利率預期在 50%以上水準，若額外選配各項高
階測試模組，單台售價可提升至 1 億至 3 億元。汎銓近年積極建立 AI 晶片分析
平台，並同步發展矽光子檢測、自主設 備開發與專利授權等新成長動能，期望

### Page 1

HD현대중공업은 전일(9/10) 대출력 발전엔진 (20MW, 9MW 급) 및 SMR 캐파 
확장을 위한 신규시설투자를 공시했다. 규모는 총 1조 722억원이며, 그 중 엔진 관련 
투자는 8,336억원, SMR 관련 투자는 2,386억원이다.

엔진 관련 투자에서는 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 확장을 위한 신규 공장 신설 및 기존 
공장 생산 설비 구축을 진행하고자 한다. 동사의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파는 기존 3GW 
규모이며, 이번 증설을 통해 신규 공장 캐파 3GW, 기존 공장 증설분 1.2GW가 
더해져 2028년 5월 기준 최종적으로 7.2GW 규모가 될 것으로 예상된다. 영암 
공장과 신축 공장에서는 데이터센터향으로 납품될 9MW 및 20MW 규모의 대출력 
엔진 위주, 기존 울산 공장은 3~5MW 규모의 선박용 엔진 위주의 생산이 이루어질 
계획이다. 이번 공장 신축 완료 이후에도 가용 부지가 남아있어 시장 수요에 따라 
향후 추가적인 캐파 확대 역시 고려할 수 있다.

SMR 투자의 경우 현대건설/테라파워와의 협력을 위한 SMR 주기기 연 2기 생산 
캐파 확보를 목적으로 진행되었다. 1기 당 출력 용량은 345MW이며, 구체적인 생산 
단가 및 물량의 경우 테라파워사와 지속 협의 중으로 동사의 미래 먹거리로서 
중장기적인 성장성에 기여할 전망이다.

데이터센터향 수요 성장에 대한 확신은 실제 엔진 업체들의 수주 인콰이어리 및 신규 
투자에서 드러나고 있다. 엔진 발전 시장 상위 업체 바르질라와 에버런스가 올해 
결정한 증설 규모는 총 4.5GW로 파악되며, 동사 역시 같은 행보로 신규 시장에서 
유의미한 점유율을 확보할 것으로 기대된다. 동사의 데이터센터향 4행정 중속엔진 
수주 단가를 고려했을 때 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 가능하다. 또한, 현재 동사 
엔진사업부의 캐시카우를 담당하고 있는 2행정 저속엔진 대비 좋은 수익성을 보여줄 
것으로 기대되는 바, 데이터센터향 생산이 시작될 28년 하반기부터 점진적으로 엔진 
믹스 개선 및 마진 성장이 가속화될 것으로 전망한다.

⾼盛分析表⽰， AI 疲软重新定价了资本⽀出周期，减轻了潜在债券发⾏的压⼒， " 影响股票
的问题，正在帮助解决利率问题。 "
摘要：
AI 科技股的集中抛售、法国主权债务危机加剧与中东油价冲击三重压⼒同步引爆，美国国债市场
迎来三年来最猛烈的单⽇反弹，避险逻辑重回主导。
周四， 10 年期美债收益率单⽇下⾏ 19.3 个基点⾄ 4.952% ，重回 5% 下⽅，创 2023 年 3 ⽉硅⾕银⾏
危机以来最⼤单⽇跌幅。数据显⽰，过去⼗年仅有 15 个交易⽇出现过如此幅度的单⽇波动。与
此同时，市场对美联储加息的预期随之后移 ——联邦基⾦利率期货⽬前完全定价于明年 1 ⽉加息
25 个基点，此前市场预期的时间节点为 12 ⽉。
这轮⾏情的深层信号在于：油价飙升之际，美债同步被抢购，市场将⾼油价解读为增⻓威胁⽽
⾮通胀威胁。野村跨资产策略师 Charlie McElligott 在客⼾报告中写道， " 债券正重新发挥对冲功
能 " ， " 市场似乎正在实时重新定价 AI 重置带来的美国 ' 增⻓恐慌 ' 概率。 "

三重冲击同步引爆市场
本轮美债急涨由三条独⽴⻛险线索⼏乎同时触发。
AI 财报季表现不及市场隐性预期是⾸要导⽕索。 Lam Research 业绩强劲但未突破预期上限；特
斯拉将全年资本⽀出指引从逾 250 亿美元下调⾄约 200 亿美元； Meta 虽上调全年资本⽀出指引下
限⾄ 1350 亿美元，但这意味着仅第四季度单季⽀出即需达 490 亿⾄ 590 亿美元，市场对执⾏可⾏
性产⽣疑虑。巴克莱信⽤分析师 Andrew Keches 在财报后报告中指出，Meta 未来将同时依赖信
贷市场与股票市场， " 债券发⾏的绝对规模也可能上升 " ，这将在⼀段时间内压制其信⽤利差。
法国主权债务危机同步升温。法国 10 年期国债收益率盘中⼀度升⾄ 5.142% ，为 2002 年 6 ⽉以来
最⾼盘中⽔平；法德 10 年期利差扩⼤⾄逾 170 个基点，创 2011 年欧债危机以来最⾼；法国信⽤
违约互换突破 100 个基点，为 2012 年以来⾸次。法国 10 年期收益率⼀度⾼于意⼤利同期限收益

率约 50 个基点，属历史上从未出现的情形。 DWS 美洲固收主管 George Catrambone 表⽰，法国
的动荡 " 带来了⼀定的避险买盘 " 。

Document 228: AI芯片需求持续释放，寒武纪Q3营收同比增长104.47%，净利润增147.74%｜财报见闻

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI芯片需求持续释放，寒武纪Q3营收同比增长104.47%，净利润增147.74%｜财报见闻.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

寒武纪 Q3 营收 35.31 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 104.47% ；归⺟净利润 14.04 亿元，同⽐增⻓
147.74% 。前三季度营收、净利润分别增⻓ 106.76% 和 131.49% ，公司称增⻓主要得益于
持续拓展市场、推动⼈⼯智能应⽤落地。与此同时，存货增⾄ 116.65 亿元，预付款项和应
付票据也⼤幅增加。
摘要：
寒武纪三季度营收、净利润继续保持⾼速增⻓，⼈⼯智能应⽤落地带动业务规模进⼀步扩⼤。
10 ⽉ 23 ⽇，寒武纪披露 2026 年第三季度报告，第三季度实现营业收⼊ 35.31 亿元，同⽐增⻓
104.47% ；归属于上市公司股东的净利润为 14.04 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 147.74% 。
前三季度，公司累计实现营业收⼊ 95.26 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 106.76% ；归⺟净利润 37.15 亿元，同
⽐增⻓ 131.49% 。营收和利润增速均超过 100% ，净利润增速⾼于营收增速，盈利规模进⼀步扩
⼤。
寒武纪在财报中表⽰，营收增⻓主要得益于持续拓展市场、积极助⼒⼈⼯智能应⽤落地。随着

AI 算⼒需求增⻓及相关应⽤逐步推进，公司业务规模持续扩张。
与此同时，公司资产负债表也出现明显变化。截⾄三季度末，总资产达到 223.64 亿元，较年初
增⻓ 66.43% ；存货、预付款项和应付票据均⼤幅增加，反映出业务扩张伴随采购备货及供应链
结算规模上升。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

营收与利润保持⾼增⻓，主营业务盈利能⼒提升
2026 年前三季度，寒武纪实现营业收⼊ 95.26 亿元，较上年同期的 46.07 亿元增⻓ 106.76% 。公
司将营收增⻓归因于持续拓展市场、积极助⼒⼈⼯智能应⽤落地。
盈利⽅⾯，前三季度归⺟净利润为 37.15 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 131.49% ；扣除⾮经常性损益后的净利
润为 35.23 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 148.30% ，增速⾼于归⺟净利润。第三季度，归⺟净利润和扣⾮净利
润分别同⽐增⻓ 147.74% 和 168.13% 。
前三季度，公司⾮经常性损益合计约 1.91 亿元，其中政府补助约 1.23 亿元，⾦融资产公允价值变
动及处置收益约 5552 万元。扣⾮净利润增速⾼于归⺟净利润，表明扣除⾮经常性损益后的利润
增⻓更快。
此外，前三季度扣除股份⽀付影响后的净利润为 39.05 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 120.32% 。这⼀指标反映
了股份⽀付费⽤对当期利润的影响，与会计⼝径下的归⺟净利润有所不同。
研发投⼊持续增加，经营现⾦流仍有波动

研发⽅⾯，寒武纪第三季度研发投⼊为 5.23 亿元，同⽐增⻓ 73.66% ；前三季度累计投⼊ 12.25 亿
元，同⽐增⻓ 45.35% 。由于营收增速⾼于研发投⼊增速，前三季度研发投⼊占营收的⽐例降⾄
12.86% ，同⽐下降 5.43 个百分点。研发投⼊总额仍在增⻓，公司继续加⼤技术研发⼒度。

截⾄三季度末，寒武纪总资产为 223.64 亿元，较年初增⻓ 66.43% ；归属于上市公司股东的净资
产为 151.24 亿元。资产规模扩⼤之际，公司营运资⾦相关科⽬也出现明显变化。

资产端，存货从年初的约 49.43 亿元增⾄ 116.65 亿元，预付款项从 7.45 亿元增⾄ 30.44 亿元。负
债端，应付票据从 3.51 亿元增⾄ 49.66 亿元，应付账款从 7.65 亿元增⾄ 18.27 亿元，合同负债则
从约 61 万元增⾄ 1.41 亿元。
上述变化表明，公司采购备货及供应链结算规模明显扩⼤。存货增加有助于为后续⽣产和交付
提供资源保障，但也意味着更多资⾦沉淀于库存。未来存货能否顺利转化为收⼊和现⾦流，将
成为观察公司经营质量的重要指标。
与此同时，公司资产负债率从年初的 11.87% 升⾄ 32.36% 。尽管归属于上市公司股东的净资产仍
达 151.24 亿元，但随着存货、预付款项及应付票据增加，后续仍需关注库存消化、供应商结算
及经营现⾦流的变化。

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

1 
Topics  Loss‑making firms are on the rise in China's growth sectors

In China, the number of companies operating in the red is increasing. And the rise is particularly striking in growth 
sectors. One factor behind the increase is the intense competition among firms, which has led to a situation in which 
only the strongest thrive. Another is that many next‑generation growth companies, despite being viewed as having great 
potential, are losing money.

■ Loss‑making firms are on the rise in growth sectors 
In recent years, the number of companies in China's 
growth sectors that are operating in the red has 
increased markedly. The share of loss-making firms in 
all listed firms (below, "loss‑making‑firm ratio") rose 
sharply from 9.6% in 2019 to 26.9% in 2025. Looking 
at the industry distribution of loss‑making firms, from 
2019 to 2022, the ratio increased mainly in 
non‑manufacturing sectors such as real estate and 
accommodation and eating/drinking services, 
reflecting the property market downturn and strict 
stay‑at‑home measures implemented to curb the spread 
of COVID‑19. Later, from 2022 to 2025, the 
loss‑making-firm ratio surged in 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices; general, production, and 
commercial machinery; electrical machine ry; and 
chemicals. These four sectors alone accounted for 
around 60% of the overall increase. 
They are the principal growth sectors in Chinese 
manufacturing. This is evidenced by the fact that the 
growth rates of value added in all four sectors exceed 
the average for Chinese manufacturing. Many of the 
high‑tech manufacturing industries that the Chi nese 
government has prioritized for development are 
included in these sectors. For example, 5G base 
stations and semiconductors fall under 
information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices. And robots fall under general, 
production, and com mercial machinery. Meanwhile, 
solar cells and batteries fall under electrical machinery, 
and biopharmaceuticals and organic chemical 
materials fall under chemicals.  
Looking at recent developments in the 
manufacturing of information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices, the global 
communications standard began shifting from 4G 
(LTE) to 5G services in around 2019, and ChatGPT was released in November 2022. These developments 
have provided tailwinds for the growth of the sector. Recently, as it has become clear that ordinary 
consumers and companies can make use of AI services on a daily basis, demand for AI‑related devices and 
semiconductors, as well as d ata centers, has soared. In this environment, many firms are focusing on 
strengthening development, manufacturing, and sales, and the sector's value added has continued to 
increase, rising from 780 billion yuan in 2022 to 910 billion yuan in 2025. Yet over  the same period, the 
number of loss‑making firms increased from 113 to 198, and the loss‑making‑firm ratio jumped from 17.2% 
to 30.1%. 
■ Competition among companies means only the strongest thrive 
One reason loss‑making firms are increasing despite operating in growth sectors is the intense 
competition among companies. The fight for market share has led to overheated investment competition

### Page 3

ASIA MONTHLY REPORT  October 2026, No.307

The Japan Research Institute, Limited  
Economics Department

2 
and price competition. In information/communications 
equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing, 
the rate of growth of non -current assets from 2022 to 
2025 (+35.4%) far exceeded the growth rate of value 
added (+16.9%). This indicates that supply capacity is 
expanding faster than demand. In addition, the 
consumer price index (CPI) increased by just 0.4% 
over the same period. Service prices rose 2.2%, 
reflecting wage increases, while goods prices fell 0.7% 
due to excess supply in manufacturing. 
Information/communications equipment prices 
decreased by 3.5%, larger than the drop for goods 
overall. 
As the selection of the fittest through competition 
among companies has progressed, profits have become 
concentrated at a small number of highly competitive 
firms. The share of profits earned by the top 1% of 
firms in information/communications equipment and electronic parts/devices manufacturing rose sharply 
from 24.0% in 2022 to 43.3% in 2025. Leading companies are simultaneously establishing positions as 
both "domestic champions" and "global champions."  
■ Next‑generation growth companies are viewed as having great potential even though they 
are losing money 
However, loss‑making firms cannot simply be lumped together and labeled as losers. Globally, investors 
have shown increasing interest recently in companies with high growth potential even if they are operating 
in the red, and China has a considerable number of such firms. 
In information/communications equipment and 
electronic parts/devices manufacturing, one out of 
every two loss‑making firms is a company attracting 
strong investor interest. The Shanghai Composite 
Index rose 28.5% from 3,089 points at the beginning of 
2023 to 3,969 points at the end of 2025. 20 of the 198 
loss‑making firms in 2025 were not yet listed in 2023, 
and if these are excluded, there were 178 companies 
operating in the red as of 2025. Of these 178 firms, 96 
(53.9%) saw stock‑price increases over the period 
2023-2025 that exceeded the rise in the Shanghai 
Composite Index.  
One characteristic of these 96 companies with strong 
relative stock‑price performance is their active 
investment in R&D and intangible assets. Their 
average R&D intensity (R&D investment / sales) in 
2022 was 9.6%, and their ratio of intangible assets to 
value added stood at 60.5%. Intangible assets include 
computer software, patents, copyrights, and 
relationships with customers and suppliers. In contrast, 
the 82 loss‑making firms with weak relative 
stock‑price performance had an R&D intensity of only 
6.5% and an intangible‑asset‑to‑value‑added ratio of just 38.9%. 
As the above has shown, the increase in loss‑making firms in China's growth sectors is notable, and the 
factors behind it include not only the selection of the fittest as a result of competition among companies but 
also the emergence of next‑generation growth firms that are viewed as having great potential. 
  (Shinichi Seki)

Topics  Climate change heightens the risk of growth stagnation in Asia

Climate change could elevate the risk of economic growth flatlining in the ASEAN zone and India by causing labor 
productivity growth and capital accumulation to stall. To mitigate or avert such a scenario, establishing a business 
environment and institutions that promote "adaptation investment" by both the public and private sectors is an urgent 
task.

■ Heavy‑rain and flood damage is becoming increasingly serious in Asia 
Huge downpours and floods 
resulting in human casualties have 
been a frequent occurrence in Asia in 
the past several years. Still fresh in the 
memory is the debris flow that 
occurred in August this year near the 
border between Nepal and China, 
which as of t he time of writing has 
been recognized as a major disaster 
with thousands dead or missing. And 
in November of last year, Southeast 
Asia and South Asia saw 
record‑breaking torrential rains, 
resulting in numerous casualties, 
particularly in Indonesia and Sri  
Lanka. 
One factor that heightens the risks 
of such floods and heavy rains is 
climate change. As temperatures rise, 
the amount of water vapor in the 
atmosphere increases. Therefore, 
global warming could intensify 
torrential rainfall. Although such risk 
is shared a cross Asia, the way its 
effects are felt differs by region. 
Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and 
India are heavily affected by 
monsoons (seasonal winds), with 
many areas experiencing concentrated 
rainfall during the wet season. In such 
areas, warming can furthe r intensify 
monsoon‑driven heavy rains, 
increasing the risks of floods and 
landslides.  
■ Climate change will delay the escape from the "middle‑income trap" 
Climate change not only results in human casualties by way of natural disasters, but may also undermine 
the long‑term economic growth potential of ASEAN and India through various channels. The Asian 
Development Bank (ADB) [2024] conducted a quantitative an alysis of the extent to which climate change 
will weigh on Asian economies over the long term. Based on the IPCC's "very high GHG emissions" 
scenario (SSP5‑8.5), the analysis assumes a 4.7°C rise in the Earth's average temperature by 2100 relative 
to pre-industrial levels, and estimates the difference in real GDP between that and a hypothetical scenario 
without climate change. The results illustrate the long‑term impact on economic growth through declines 
in labor productivity, stagnation in capital accumulation, and downw ard pressure on consumption due to 
sea‑level rise, floods, heat waves, and other phenomena caused by climate change.  
An examination of the results reveals that in every country/territory, the pushdown effect on real GDP 
relative to the no‑climate‑change scenario expands over time, with 2035, 2050, and 2070 each seeing 
greater impact. The pushdown effect for ASEAN and Ind ia is −15.0% to −8.8% in 2050 and −30.2% to

4 
−18.1% in 2070, larger than the effects for China (2050: −4.8%, 2070: −10.3%) and Central Asian countries 
(2050: −1.7%, 2070: −3.8%). Within Asia, the ASEAN zone and India can be considered regions where the 
risk of economic stagnation due to climate change is relatively large.  
This may make it even more difficult for ASEAN countries and India to escape the "middle‑income trap." 
Although their income levels have risen, they still remain far from high‑income status. Indonesia, Malaysia, 
the Philippines, and Thailand became middle‑income countries in the 1990s or earlier, but have still not 
been able to join the groups of high‑income countries. Furthermore, Vietnam and India, which became 
middle‑income countries in the 2000s or later, have yet to enter a high‑growth trajectory like China once 
did. Climate change, by causing labor productivity growth to stall amid rising temperatures and companies 
to curtail investment due to more frequent disasters, may lead to income growth in the ASEAN zone and 
India being delayed further.  
■ Improving the environment to accelerate "adaptation investment" will be important 
To alleviate the pushdown effect of climate change on growth, expansion of "adaptation investment" by 
the public and private sectors is essential. Specifically, this could include improving working environments 
and promoting measures to lighten workloads a nd reduce headcounts in order to offset declines in labor 
productivity caused by rising temperatures, as well as developing infrastructure and strengthening facilities 
to prevent damage to production equipment from sea‑level rise and floods. However, ASEAN  and India 
cannot be said to have sufficiently 
developed the foundations for public 
and private promotion of such 
adaptation investment.  
As part of its Global Adaptation 
Initiative (ND‑GAIN), the University of 
Notre Dame (U.S.) evaluates countries' 
ability to use investment to achieve 
climate‑change adaptation in the form of 
a "readiness" score with three 
components: economic readiness, 
governance readiness, and social 
readiness. Respectively, these 
components assess 1) whether a 
business environment conducive to 
adaptation investment by private -sector 
companies has been put in place, 2) 
whether the effectiveness of institutions

5 
and policies to support private-sector adaptation investment has been secured, and 3) whether tangible and 
intangible social infrastructure that promotes investment has been established. 
Looking at readiness in 2024, ASEAN countries and India generally rank in the middle to lower tiers 
globally, with a large gap between them and developed countries. Notably, the Philippines, India, and 
Thailand fall outside the top 100, indicating weak momentum for adaptation investment. Breaking down 
the scores, problems are especially evident in the economic and governance categories. India and Thailand 
have particularly low eco nomic readiness, ranking 113th and 124th, respectively, and five countries 
(Malaysia not included) fall outside the top 100 for governance readiness. Given this situation, to accelerate 
private‑sector adaptation investment, India and Thailand in particular  need to move swiftly to improve the 
financial environment to make it easier to procure necessary funds domestically and internationally, relax 
regulations to encourage the launch of new businesses, and reform labor markets to enable the smooth 
securing of human resources. In addition, for Asia as a whole, it will also be important to boost confidence 
in the institutional factors that enhance application of investment for adaptation by increasing policy 
stability, ensuring the effectiveness of legal systems, and curbing corruption.

(Tomohiro Hosoi)

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 26-30
Date:    39 minutes ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: October 26-30

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
October 26-30
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 24 Oct 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 0.5% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 0.7%, both
posting second straight weekly losses. The Nasdaq’s 2.9% slide on
Thursday was its biggest one-day drop since June, as concerns flared
over the cost and availability of financial capital for AI investment. The
price of WTI crude topped $100 a barrel amid US-Iran conflict concerns,
while the 10-year Treasury yield fell to 5.0%.
The FOMC meeting (Wed) is the focus this week, alongside the release of

Q3 GDP and September's PCED inflation report (Thu). San Francisco Fed
President Mary Daly and St. Louis Fed President Alberto Musalem speak
on Friday. A heavy week of Q3 earnings reports rounds out a packed
calendar. Here’s more:
(1) FOMC. The FOMC’s two-day meeting concludes Wednesday, followed
by Fed Chair Kevin Warsh’s press conference. The CME FedWatch Tool
puts the odds of a hike this week at 9% and the odds of one in December
at 84%. Federal funds futures imply 2.7 rate hikes over the next 12

months and 1.4 over the next six (chart). We believe the committee should
hike on Wednesday but won't because the midterm elections are so close.

### Page 4

(2) GDP & PCED. The first estimate of Q3 GDP (Thu) follows Q2’s third
estimate of 2.2% saar, with the GDP price index up 6.1%. The Atlanta
Fed’s GDPNow model estimates Q3 growth at 3.5% as of October 20, led
by a 17.8% jump in business equipment spending (chart).
September’s core PCED (Thu) follows August’s 3.0% y/y. That was well
below September’s 4.6% pace of core PPI final demand for personal
consumption and above the core CPI’s 2.6% (chart). The Cleveland Fed’s

Inflation Nowcasting model projects September’s core and headline PCED
at 3.1% and 3.6% y/y, up from August’s 3.0% and 3.4%. (The model’s
m/m rates are 0.28% and 0.47%.)
CPI computer software and accessories rose 28.4% y/y in September and
PPI electronic components 26.8%, while PCED computers and peripheral

### Page 5

equipment rose just 8.4% in August. September’s PCED will show
whether that gap starts to close.
(3) ADP & unemployment claims. ADP’s weekly employment reading
(Tue) follows a drop in the four-week average to 18,500 for the week
ended October 3, down from 26,000 the prior week (chart). That’s still
more than double July’s low of 8,250, pointing to a solid October payrolls
report.
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week’s 194,000, below expectations

and near the lowest level since the late 1960s. The four-week average
stands at 197,800 (chart). Continuing claims rose to 1,728,000 for the
week ended October 9, up from 1,710,000, while their four-week average
edged down to 1,708,000.

### Page 6

(4) Regional business surveys. The Richmond Fed’s manufacturing
index (Tue) follows September’s -2 reading. The regional M-PMI business
activity index slipped to 13.4 in September from August’s 18.7, while the
national M-PMI eased to 54.5 from 54.6 (chart).
(5) Earnings. The earnings reporting season heats up this week, with
36% of the S&P 500 companies set to report. Boeing, Seagate, and
Corning report on Tuesday. Alphabet, Microsoft, SK Hynix, and GE

Vernova follow on Wednesday. Samsung, Intel, Eli Lilly, SanDisk, and
Amazon report on Thursday. Exxon Mobil reports on Friday.
Analysts’ consensus estimates imply Q3-2026 operating EPS growth for
S&P 500 companies of 24.1% y/y, slightly up from last week (chart). Q4
estimates have also risen.

### Page 7

(6) Global central banks. Both the Reserve Bank of New Zealand and
the Bank of Canada (Wed) are expected to hike by 25bps. The markets
are pricing in 88% odds that the RBNZ will lift its official cash rate to
3.00% and an 85% chance that the BOC will raise its policy rate to 2.50%.
The European Central Bank (Thu) and the Bank of Japan (Fri) are set to
hold their policy rates at 2.50% and 1.25%, respectively (chart).
December is the focus for the latter pair. The markets are pricing in 75%

odds of an ECB hike in December and a 74% probability of a BOJ hike.
Guidance from ECB President Christine Lagarde and BOJ Governor
Kazuo Ueda will show whether those odds hold.

Document 289: Federal Reserve preview_ A pause before a December hike

Source PDF: all report pdf/Federal Reserve preview_ A pause before a December hike.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-23
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Despite hot inﬂation prints in September and the Fed having
signalled that further action to tame inﬂation will likely be
needed, markets and economists are united in expecting the
central bank to leave interest rates on hold at the 28 October
FOMC meeting. Ongoing concerns about elevated price
pressures mean we do expect a December interest rate rise

James Knightley, Padhraic Garvey, CFA and Chris Turner
The Fed looks set to hold rates steady at the October FOMC meeting before
hiking again in December
Fed ofﬁcials play for time
The immediate reaction to the surprise September 0.3% month-on-
month core CPI print was to send the chances of an October rate
hike above 50%. But this didn’t last long. Earlier comments from
Federal Reserve Vice Chairs Philip Jeﬀerson and Michelle Bowman
and Governor Chris Waller, signalling no rush to decide on the next
policy move, quickly calmed markets. The fact that Fed Chair Kevin
Warsh subsequently chose not to talk up the chances of action
when he spoke at the IMF meetings in Bangkok, unlike at the

### Page 2

Jackson Hole Conference before the September hike, further
dampened the perceived chances of action.
While it is true to say that recent jobs numbers have been
disappointing and interest rate-sensitive sectors, such as the
housing market, are experiencing increasing pain, the economy is
growing robustly, and inﬂation remains above target. Fed oﬃcials
themselves have made it clear that they think more tightening of
monetary policy is going to be required to bring inﬂation to heel.

Given that the latest Federal Reserve Summary of Economic
Projections showed four FOMC members projecting 50bp of rate
hikes between now and the end of the year, the decision is unlikely
to be unanimous. Beth Hammack, Neel Kashkari and Lorie Logan
all voted to hike in July when the rest of the committee wanted to
hold steady. All three could again dissent in favour of hiking.
The press release is likely to be brief, and we also expect another
short and snappy press conference from Chair Warsh. He's likely to

keep things quite matter-of-fact given his lack of enthusiasm for
forward guidance. We expect his message to be that the economy
remains in decent shape, unemployment is low, and inﬂation is
above target. The policy changes being made will be determined
by the trends in incoming data, with oﬃcials currently thinking that
they probably have more work to do.

Looking towards December
We will have two jobs reports and one inﬂation print ahead of the
December decision. The November CPI will be released the day after
the Fed meeting, and oﬃcials will almost certainly get to see it. As
such, they will have more information than the market does,

meaning a surprise can’t be completely ruled out.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate hike at the December meeting
(leaving the Fed funds target rate range at 4-4.25%) followed by
a prolonged pause through to December 2027, when we have that
December 2026 hike being reversed. This is in line with the current
consensus forecast, while the Fed’s own projections (its central
forecast) see one hike this year with rates left unchanged
throughout 2027.
While the sharp move higher in Treasury yields and the ﬁrmer

dollar are tightening ﬁnancial conditions to a degree, our forecast
path for the Fed assumes that in the couple of months following
the midterm elections we see an agreement that improves the ﬂow
of energy from the Persian Gulf, but signiﬁcantly, also allows for a
sharp reduction in shipping and insurance costs. It's the latter point
that is crucial to delivering lower pump prices at a time when
reﬁneries are working at capacity. If correct, this should

meaningfully ease inﬂation pressures, and with US wage growth
(the biggest cost input for corporate America) looking very benign
at just 3% and the largest component of the inﬂation basket –
housing at 35% by weight – set to experience more disinﬂation, we

### Page 4

believe overall US inﬂation can fall back to 2% in the second half of
2027.
The risk to our view is clearly tilted towards a longer-lasting conﬂict
rather than one that is resolved imminently. This means we would
not rule out additional hike(s) in 1Q 2027, but with households
experiencing a greater squeeze on spending power (weak wage
growth amid rising energy costs) and bond yields likely pushing
even higher – leading to new cycle highs for mortgage rates and

corporate borrowing costs – the risks of a weaker economy will
increase. As such, in this more stressed economic scenario,
eventual cuts to policy rates will likely be swifter and more
substantial in 2H 2027 than in our base case.
The Fed to leave well enough alone and let
Treasuries do their own thing
A solid performance for US Treasuries in the past week has acted to
reduce the prior tightening impulse that had gripped the market.
The 10yr yield below 5% obviously contrasts dramatically with the

prior break above 5.25%. We’re not convinced the more recent
downward lurch is persisting. But its genesis lends itself to a softer
approach from the FOMC at this meeting.
The risk-oﬀ tone creates a heightened risk proﬁle for the US
economy, raising the probability that the Fed holds at this meeting.
The battering that the French bond market has been taking is
another important impulse, as it heightens the broader sense of
bond angst. And at the margin, it can add to systemic pressure.

This is not the type of environment where the Fed would be inclined
to add to concerns by tightening policy. It’s the perfect meeting to
keep rates on hold.
Whether we remain below 5% for the 10yr yield is a whole other
question. We're minded to expect it to snap back above, as we still
have inﬂation, ﬁscal deﬁcit, and AI-boom impulses at play. The Fed
will no doubt remind us of these ongoing pressures. Plus, a break
back above 5% will likely need a degree of calm in the risk space

ﬁrst. We think it should ultimately move back above 5%; the timing,

### Page 5

however, is a tougher call. That being said, this is not the meeting
to rock the boat on the bond market.
We’re likely still some way from the Fed spelling out a plan to shrink
its balance sheet, which we know remains a policy objective for
Chair Warsh. We expect recommendations from the various
committees to surface closer to the turn of the year. Meanwhile,
liquidity circumstances are stable, with repo behaving far better
than it did a year ago.

Warsh FOMC meetings have set the dollar
tone since June
The dollar should be able to hold on to most of its September
gains, as Fed communication is likely to remain supportive of
higher US yields. If the dollar is to correct lower, a renewed tech-led
risk-oﬀ move rather than a dovish shift in Fed messaging is the
more likely catalyst.
After a sharp 4% run higher through September and into early
October, the dollar has entered a more consolidative phase. Rate

diﬀerentials are the most important driver at the moment, and
these have moved slightly against the dollar this month as the US
tech sell-oﬀ has pared back Fed tightening expectations.
Broadening out the lens, it looks like Warsh-led FOMC meetings
have set the four-to-six-week trend for the dollar this year. June’s
meeting was hawkish, July’s was seen as dovish, and the Fed
delivered a hawkish hike in September.
We suspect that Warsh has learned from the criticism he received

over July's FOMC meeting, where he implied that the bond market
was doing the tightening (read as the Fed would not have to). That
probably means he will not be drawn into concluding that wider
credit spreads and softer equity prices mean tighter ﬁnancial
conditions now require fewer Fed hikes.
Assuming that Warsh sticks to the script that monetary policy
credibility remains the Fed’s top priority, short-dated US yields can
remain well-supported, and the dollar can largely hold on to the

gains it has made since early September. And the euro looks like it

► 美股 走 高，主 要 受 惠科 技 與 半導 體 表 現突
出，費城半導體指數漲勢尤其明顯 。雖然近
一週油價回落 ，稍微舒緩市場對能源通膨的
擔憂，但聯準會如預期升息1碼，後續表現動
能可能受壓抑 。此外，最新利率預測顯示 ，
官員們認為年底前至少還有一次升息 ，主因
通膨偏高 、經濟與勞動市場保持韌性 ，因此

升息可能仍有必要。
► 日股表現受惠大型科技 、半導體設備及 AI概
念股，且日本央行雖升息但並無更鷹派的態
度，日圓走弱並激勵出口類股走勢 。歐股則
缺乏明確方向 ，雖科技股彈升及油價回落提
供支撐 ，然而全球央行緊縮及公債殖利率走
升壓抑估值 ，且法國財政疑慮亦推升風險溢
價，德國因執政聯盟主要政黨在地方選舉遭
遇挫敗，增加了政策與政治上的不確定性。
► 產業部分 ， 除了有 AI 題材帶動的科技及通
訊，工業類股亦有不錯表現 ，其餘類股則漲
跌互見。

► 美國9月23日公布的 9月製造業及服務業 PMI

勢暫時止穩 。黃金維持震盪格局 ，受到殖利

率、地緣衝突及油價等多空因素交錯影響。

► 隨人工智慧運算需求持續攀升 ，谷歌、亞馬
遜、臉書、微軟及OpenAI等企業巨頭積極發
展客製化晶片 (ASIC)，藉此提升運算效率並
優化成 本 。根據 凱 基投顧 引述產 業數 據預
估，2027年客製化晶片出貨量將年增 92%至
1,446萬顆 ，首度超 越繪圖處理器 (GPU)的
1,129萬顆 ，顯示AI晶片市場正由 GPU主導
逐步轉向多元化發展。
► 在客製化晶片方面 ，谷歌持續擴大自研張量
處理器 (TPU)的應用與對外供應 ，亞馬遜亦
積極拓展自研AI晶片(Trainium)的雲端運算服
務，預估兩者合計將佔 2027年客製化晶片出
貨量約80%。
► 隨客製 化晶 片需 求 快速增 加 ，相 關晶 片設
計、先 進製 程及 先 進封裝 需求可 望同 步提
升，涉及上述業務發展的台股供應鏈 (註)有機
會受到市場關注。

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 2024年全球工廠運作的工業機器人約 470萬
台，為10年前的 3倍，主要為韓國 、中國和
日本等製造業大國將其運用於汽車 、電子和
金屬加工等產業 ，並因而維持成本和品質優
勢，為競爭力重要來源之一 。雖然美國工業
機器人的普及程度仍低於亞洲主要製造業國
家，但近年積極尋求重建工業產能 、將關鍵
供應鏈遷回美國國內以及擴大半導體和製藥
生產，有可能讓美國逐步調整工業和製造層
面的策略方向。
► 傳統工業機器人與人形機器人在外觀設計與
應用場景上有明顯差異 。工業機器人通常部
署於高度標準化的環境 ，以執行範圍相對有
限且重複性高的特定作業為主 ，而人形機器
人則被設計用於多變的環境 ，並經常需要與
人類協同工作 ，因此需結合移動能力 、感知
能力與靈巧操作能力 ，才能夠執行多樣化任
務，而非受限單一功能。
工業機器人技術相對成熟且部署速度較快全球運作中的工業機器人數量穩定成長
工業機器人技術成熟且穩定成長，為製造業強國的重要競爭力來源

### Page 7

7
投資焦點人形機器人
► 人 形機 器 人 主 要由 結 合半 導 體 與AI 模 型的
「大腦」，並由感測器 、執行器 、線材、連
接器及輕量化電池驅動的機身所組成 ，可透
過感知環境資訊 、思考並進行協調的實體動
作。投行預估至2030年，執行器將佔整體人
形機器人成本超過 50%，為價值含量最高的
核心零組件 ，麥肯錫公司甚至認為執行器與
感測器可能成為未來供應鏈中的關鍵瓶頸。
► 機器人在實體世界中執行任務時 ，任何延遲
或連線中斷都可能帶來重大風險 ，因此相較
純數位的 AI應用 ，對即時運算 (邊緣運算 )能
力的要求更高 。而近幾年邊緣運算軟硬體能
力大幅提升 ，使本地端設備得以部署數十億
參數的 AI模型 ，並將反應延遲縮短至約 0.3
秒，使機器人能夠即時感知 、決策與行動 。
此外，過去6年風險資本對機器人、感測器及
IT 硬體的每年平均投資約 100 億美元 ， 但
2026年僅上半年即已達 280億美元 ，暗示市
場對機器人產業潛力的期待有別於過往。
機器人技術進展快速並開始吸引風險資本投資人形機器人運作有賴AI模型與大量機身零組件整合

人形機器人產業結合AI與硬體，含金量高吸引風險資本進駐

8
股市
◆ 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，公債殖利率走高壓抑股債市場近期動能 。然而包括油價自高檔回落使
通膨壓力稍緩 ，以及美股第三季獲利年增率仍為雙位數成長 ，股市表現有支撐 。市場持續觀察AI資
本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場動盪 ，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降
低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評價面壓力等紛擾帶來負面影響 ，可布局防
禦股抗震，長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
◆ 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
◆ 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨發展 ，加上9月製造業及服務業PMI優於預期，殖利率偏高環境建議

以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流
與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
◆ 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
◆ 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
◆ 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
◆ 中東地緣衝突加劇推升油價，短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
◆ 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維

### Page 15

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

### Page 1

Westpac New Zealand Economics
• Higher oil and refined fuels prices, a lower
exchange rate, higher long term interest rates
and weak house price trends justify a review of
our 2026 forecasts.
• We continue to see CPI inflation falling to 3.7%
y/y in the September quarter, but now think
inflation will rise to 4.2% in the December
quarter. Higher global fuel prices and a weaker
New Zealand dollar are driving these trends.
• Commodity export prices remain robust and

are reflected in a rise in our forecast for the
2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price to $9.75/kg. Our
opening bid for the Milk Price in 2027/28 is a
solid $9.50/kg.
• House prices have not stabilised as expected
in recent months. As a result, we have revised
down our 2026 house price forecast from a
small rise to a 1.4% decline.
• The combination of high fuel costs and a weak
housing market will likely crimp consumer
demand running into Christmas.
• We have modestly revised down our GDP

forecasts for the December quarter, which
reduces our 2026 growth forecast to a still
solid 2.5%. Risks appear skewed to the
downside given higher energy prices and long-
term interest rates.
• We will look at our 2027 forecasts in the
coming week.
• Our OCR forecasts remain unchanged. We see
a pause at the October meeting and a hike to
3% at the release of the December Monetary
Policy Statement.
Inflation – All gas, no brakes.
We’ve updated our near-term inflation forecasts in

response to recent global developments.
We continue to estimate that consumer prices rose by
0.7% in the September quarter, consistent with recent
monthly prices updates from Stats NZ. That would see
annual inflation ease to 3.7% in the year to September,
down from 4.1% in the year to June. We’ll confirm that
figure next week, when the final monthly prices update
for the September quarter is released on 16 October.

However, that easing in inflation appears set to be only
a temporary reprieve. We now expect consumer prices
to rise 1.1% in the December quarter. That would see
annual inflation rising to a three-year high of 4.2% by

the end of this year. The main driver of this revision are
sharply higher global fuel prices, compounded by a more
than 4% fall in the New Zealand dollar since the start of
September. Together, those developments have already
pushed local petrol prices up around 50 cents / litre over
the past month, with the nationwide average price of 91
unleaded reaching a record $3.53 / litre earlier this week.

RBNZ target range
Our updated year-end inflation forecast is above the
RBNZ’s September projection of 3.9%. In a recent
presentation, Governor Breman noted that the rise in oil
prices meant the risks to the RBNZ’s near-term inflation
forecast were to the upside. However, she also noted that

the RBNZ’s focus was on the medium-term outlook.
We haven’t changed our quarterly inflation forecasts for
2027 at this stage. However, the upward revision to our
forecast for the December quarter means annual inflation
is set to remain firm through most of 2027, consistent
with the RBNZ’s forecasts. We will review our 2027
forecasts for inflation and growth more comprehensively
next week when we will also review our financial forecasts
with the release of the Westpac Market Outlook.

A more tepid housing market.
We had expected the housing market to stabilise through
the second half of 2026. However, recent trends have
disappointed. House sales volumes continue to weaken,
and the median number of days required to sell a house
has lengthened to 48 days. Not surprisingly, therefore,

average house prices have continued to nudge lower
despite more robust conditions in those areas – mostly
in the South Island – that are most exposed to the well-
performing export sector. The latest indicators suggest
no imminent change in this trend.
Given these weak trends, our previous forecast for
essentially flat house prices through the September and
December quarters now looks too optimistic. We have
revised down our house price forecast for the 2026 year

from a rise of 0.6% y/y to a fall of 1.4% y/y on a national
basis. This is consistent with quarterly falls in house
prices of 0.5% and 0.8% q/q over the September and
December quarters.
The still weak labour market, higher mortgage rates and
still moderate employment growth all point to a weak
housing market for the foreseeable future. Risks remain
tilted towards another weak year in 2027. This would
especially be the case if increased taxation of capital

gains on investor housing were to occur as a result of the
outcome of the 2026 General Election.

It’s likely to be the case that there will be divergent trends
in the housing market across the country. Income and
employment trends in tourism and agriculture dominated

regions are expected to remain robust. Our upgrade to
the 2026/27 Milk Price released in our recent Agri Bites
report emphasises this point. But it looks like it will be
a while before investors and homeowners in the major
metropolitan areas in the North Island will be feeling
wealthier through a rising home value. On balance this
is likely to crimp consumer spending attitudes through
the end of 2026 and might moderate the recovery in the
construction sector that is underway.

Growth forecasts moderated a touch.
Our short-term growth forecasts still appear to be in
the ballpark of what seems likely given recent trends.
Recent consumer and business confidence surveys have

suggested resilience of the economy to recent rises in
energy prices and long-term interest rates. However, it’s
likely the rise in energy prices in particular will eat into
consumer spending appetites towards the end of 2026
should current levels of fuel prices be sustained.
We have knocked off a modest amount off our forecast
for consumer spending in the December quarter to
reflect these risks and will look more closely at the
implications for 2027 growth in coming weeks. Certainly,

the large rise in long term interest rates may be relevant
for 2027 growth trends. The election outcome introduces
two-sided risks to the 2027 growth outlook that are hard
to quantify at this early stage. We currently expect 2.5%
y/y growth for the 2026 year.
We don’t see a need to change our labour market
forecasts for 2026 at this point. Signs continue to
accumulate suggesting strengthening employment
growth – although it will be a while before a noticeable

drop in the unemployment rate is evident.
Implications for the OCR.
We are leaving our forecasts of the OCR unchanged.
Since the July 2026 Monetary Policy Review, we have
anticipated the RBNZ will slow the pace of OCR increases
now the 3% neutral level (in the RBNZ’s view) is coming
into view.
The RBNZ sent a clear signal that they expected to pause
in October and raise the OCR just one more time in
December. We still think this is the most likely scenario.

The recent data has boosted the short-term inflation
profile but at this stage there is little evidence of rising
inflation expectations or more persistent inflationary
pressures. There are some risks to the growth outlook –
albeit two sided – to consider. Taking the time to assess
the impact of the OCR rises to date and the strategy going
forward makes sense to us. We don’t see a shift in the
MPC’s strategy in October.
We continue to think it most likely that the RBNZ will

have more work to do in 2027. However, there is a lot
of water to go under the bridge before then. The OCR
increases that have occurred to date, and the one further
expected increase in December will position the MPC
well to respond to conditions in 2027 as they unfold.
The unexpected rise in long term interest rates that
has occurred in the last 6 weeks or so has tightened
monetary conditions and has at least offset the easing in
conditions coming from the weaker exchange rate over

the same period.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

India: RBI monetary policy committee 
meeting (7-Oct)

The macro environment has undergone 
significant changes since the RBI 
monetary policy committee’s (MPC) 
August policy meeting, with global oil 
prices rising past $100bl, and US Fed 
kickstarting their rate hike cycle. 
Domestically, core and headline 
continued to rise, laying the ground for 
inflation to quicken beyond 5.8 -6.0% in 
the second half as price risks broaden. 
Strong 1QFY27 GDP growth meanwhile 
pointed to the economy’s relative 
resilience to external shocks.

Besides fuel feeding into food costs, 
weather has also proved to be disruptive, 
with indications that the El Niño 
phenomenon could extend into 2027. 
International food benchmarks, including 
the UN Food Price index and the 
Bloomberg spot agricultural indice s, 
continue to climb. India’s food inflation 
has become more broad -based, shifting 
beyond vegetables and pulses to sugar, 
milk, protein groups, and edible oils. Sub-
par weather has contributed to this 
upmove, besides idiosyncratic factors 
like swing in production trends (cobweb), 
diversion for fuel usage etc.

After two years of normal monsoon, 
cumulative southwest rainfall this year is 
set to end with rainfall around 12 -13% 
below the long -term average, the most 
since 2015 and resulting in a slower 
build-up in reservoir levels.  
Global benchmark oil price gauge has 
averaged ~$93pb in FY27 year -to-date, 
up 36% yoy  vs FY26. Retail pump fuel 
prices were adjusted in quick succession 
in May26 and have been left unchanged 
since then, while other fuel products 
including CNG, aviation fuel, LPG etc 
have continued to climb. Add to this, 
other pockets like telecom, restaura nts, 
business services, transport fares etc 
have been inching up, reflecting early 
signs of broadening in price pressures (on 
sequential basis). A gradual broadening 
of price pressures is likely to keep 
headline inflation high and narrow the 
real rate buffer significantly.

Authorities are also conducting liquidity 
absorption measures through market 
operations like VRRR, open market 
operations and sell-buy FX swaps. This is 
likely to align the overnight call money 
rates to the benchmark repo rate, after 
having fallen sharply d ue to the surplus 
in the banking system.  Liquidity and core 
inflation have historically displayed a 
modest positive relationship, as per our 
study. As such, the RBI's inflation 
mandate has likely reinforced the push to 
drain excess liquidity.

We expect the RBI MPC to raise the repo 
rate by 25bp to 5.5% , citing pipeline 
inflationary pressures. Stance could be 
changed to withdrawal of 
accommodation to signal hawkish intent. 
We expect one more hike within 4Q26, 
though the odds of additional hikes have 
risen as oil prices stay stubbornly high 
and weather remains unfavourable.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect industrial production to grow 
by 5.8% yoy in August from 4.7% yoy in 
July. Strength in  export-oriented 
manufacturing is likely to persist , 
supported by sustained demand for 
artificial intelligence-related electronics 
goods, while mining output may have 
recovered from its contraction in July.

Taiwan

September trade and inflation data are 
forthcoming. Export growth is expected 
to sustain the 40% pace seen in the 
previous month. The global AI boom is 
facing rising headwinds from 
synchronised rate hikes by G3 central 
banks, higher bond yields, and rising  
financing costs. That said, there are so 
far no clear signs of a significant 
slowdown, based on the high -frequency 
indicators we track across AI demand, 
investment, and supply.

CPI is expected to rebound to 2.4% YoY 
in September, up from 2.0% in August. 
The earlier arrival of the Mid -Autumn 
Festival this year may have boosted food 
and services prices on a YoY basis. The 
rebound in global energy prices should 
also have lifted tran sport costs, 
including gasoline prices and airfares. 
While electricity tariffs will remain 
frozen, the December review implies a 
possibility of an electricity price hike 
after the local elections.

Given still -strong economic growth, 
persistent inflation, and the potential for 
inflation expectations to rise, we 
maintain our view that the central bank 
will raise the policy rate by a modest 
12.5bps to 2.125% in December.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to accelerate to 3.2% yoy in September 
from 2.5% yoy  in August, slightly 
exceeding the central bank’s 1 -3% 
inflation target. Faster increases in 
energy and food prices likely pushed 
overall inflation higher, despite an 
easing in electricity tariffs starting 
September. We expect the Bank of 
Thailand (BOT), w hich views the rise in 
inflation as largely supply -driven, to 
keep its policy rate stable at 1.00% for 
the remainder of 2026, aiming to support 
an economy with low and uneven growth.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Markets have had little respite over the past month. Hopes 
of a lasting US–Iran agreement have faded and intermittent 
attacks on shipping have kept Brent oil prices in triple digits. 
Workarounds have allowed a meaningful share of crude to 
continue flowing but these arrangements are slower and 
costlier, while depleted inventories leave little room to 
absorb another shock and a range of disruptions to refining 
and shipping are also impacting downstream energy costs. 
Bond markets have been just as restless, with continued lifts 
in government bond yields across much of the globe and 
more sharply in the US. 
Notably, oil is no longer doing the heavy lifting for yields. 
The rise has been concentrated in real yields, with the Fed’s 
September hike – and its signal of more to come – acting as 
something of a trigger. In the US, fiscal concerns have been 
more of a sub-plot, though the Treasury’s efforts to hold 
down long yields have arguably only drawn more attention 
to them. 
In our view, markets are getting ahead of themselves on 
monetary policy offshore. We expect just one more hike 
from the Fed rather than the three the market currently has 
priced, with stretched housing affordability posing risks to 
both consumption and inflation. Central banks in Europe, the 
UK and Canada are also likely to bide their time after a final 
move. Europe is weathering the energy shock better than 
feared and has far more fiscal room to respond than the US – 
one reason we still see the US dollar trending lower over the 
next two years.
The RBA hiked again in September and looks set to follow 
up in November, taking the cash rate to a new cycle high. 
Some of the risks the Board flagged have crystallised, 
but the minutes also reveal a tendency to reach for the 
more hawkish read of the data, in addition to the familiar 
downbeat take on supply capacity. Our base case assumes 
no return to full-scale conflict, but with energy buffers 
depleted, bond markets less forgiving and the RBA already 
leaning hawkish, the margin for error – for markets and 
policymakers alike – is starting to ‘wear thin’.
Australia: After a soft but better than expected first half, the 
Australian economy is facing into more challenges as another 
round of disruptions from global energy markets combine with 
further interest rate rises and a deepening housing downturn. 
The demand mix should see capacity pressures continue to 
ease but with significant risks on either side.
Middle East: Low inventories and higher shipping costs will 
keep oil and fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast 
to average US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by 
Q4 2027 as supply recovers. Risks are two-sided: escalation 
could push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran 
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply.
Commodities: Most commodities rose in September, with 
many of the themes that drove moves across August remaining 
in place. Japanese LNG and Brent oil recorded strong gains 
amid Middle East tensions. Copper and aluminium extended 
recent gains while iron ore was supported by higher freight 
and production costs. Gold fell after the Fed’s September rate 
hike. 
Global FX Markets: In late September and early-October 
the DXY index popped higher to near 103, a high back to 
early-2025. Underlying the move are expectations that 
inflation will prove persistent globally as well as a belief that 
the US’ tech sector leaves it best positioned to weather the 
storm.
New Zealand: Higher fuel costs and a weaker exchange rate 
have lifted the short-term inflation outlook while the economy 
has continued to grow through the Iran war. Growth looks set 
to continue to be buoyed by strong commodity prices and a 
supportive exchange rate. However, downside risks are growing 
from higher energy costs and long-term interest rates. 
United States: The US housing market remains under 
considerable pressure. Prices continue to edge up, supporting 
wealth. But up-and-coming main street consumers are 
struggling with affordability and availability, posing enduring 
risks for both consumption growth and inflation.   
China: With each month that passes, the risk of disappointing 
authorities’ 4.5-5.0% ambition grows. While the Government 
is right to take a targeted approach to support, it must quickly 
multiply its scale if downside risks are to be kept at bay. 
Asia: For much of the past twenty years, Asia has been a 
source of disinflation for the world. But a combination of ageing 
populations, rising labour costs, increasing electricity demand 
and technology sector developments imply Asia may provide 
less support to global disinflation over the decade ahead.
Europe & UK: The euro has come under considerable pressure 
during the past month. That said, the European economy 
continues to tick along nicely while inflation risks remain 
manageable, allowing the ECB to be measured in their response.

Wearing thin

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
The main development affecting global markets this month
has been the sharp lift in selected bond yields, especially in
the US and France. Since the previous Market Outlook was
published, 10-year benchmarks have risen 27bps for US

Treasuries, to be well above the 5% threshold, and 40bps for
French bonds. The latter move contrasts with German bunds,
which have seen little change in yields at that tenor over the
month, though they are up more than 60bps year to date.
Several factors are behind the latest rise, with no single
cause dominating. For some of these factors, the underlying
conditions had been in evidence for some time, but recent
events focused attention on them. The rise has been primarily

a story of real yields: longer-run inflation expectations implied
by both breakevens and swaps have not increased that much.
For much of the year, fluctuations in oil prices – and thus the
near-term inflation outlook – fed into market views of central
bank policy, and longer yields as well. More recently, though,
this correlation has broken down. Shifts in expectations about
central bank policy have remained important for bond pricing,
as evident in the concentration of the increase in real yields.

The FOMC’s decision to increase the Fed funds rate, and flag in
the ‘dot plots’ that at least one more hike was likely, seems to
have been something of a trigger event for repricing, not only
for US yields. Also salient were the various actions taken by
the US Treasury to suppress long yields and improve the US
government’s debt-servicing position. These efforts had little
lasting effect and yields are now higher, and higher relative to
peers, than before. But they may have served to draw attention

to the growing issues around US fiscal sustainability. High
supply of debt from hyperscaler firms to fund the AI / data
centre boom has also likely pressured yields at the margin.
That said, fiscal concerns are unlikely to have been the main
issue driving the lift in US yields. The slope of the US yield
curve is noticeably flatter than at the beginning of the year;
in other words, short yields have risen even more than the
10-year yield, signifying tighter expected monetary policy

lifting yields across the curve. Another lens on this shift is that
2-year and other short-dated yields have increased more than
estimates of risk-neutral rate, such as that from the NY Fed.
Fiscal concerns have been more salient in the case of France,
where spreads to bunds have widened from around 70bps at
the beginning of the year to nearly 140bps currently, wider than
Greece or Italy, with about 40bps of the widening occurring in
the past month. Contagion to other euro area sovereigns has

been limited, with spreads widening around 22bps over the
month for Greece, Italy and Belgium, and 13bps for Spain.
Many factors lifting global bond yields ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... and RBA policy rates outlook
In Australia, the RBA hiked the cash rate again at its late
September meeting, and a further hike at the November
meeting looks likely. This would take the cash rate to 4.85%, a
new cycle high.
While the forecasts the RBA published in August did not

support further rate hikes, in September these forecasts were
characterised as resting on several things going right and
certain risks not emerging. Some of these risks, including a re-
escalation of the war in the Middle East and thus energy prices,
have since crystallised. The post-meeting communication
and minutes also pointed to the July CPI result being above
expectations, as was June quarter GDP growth “at the margin”.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction

costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data
centre boom. No weight is put on the possibility that this new
investment and technology might add to productivity or the
capital stock, and so supply capacity, even with a lag. Indeed,
the post-meeting minutes highlighted the financial stability
risks if valuations in this area should disappoint “even if the
technology were ultimately successful”, as if the latter was not
yet clear. This language points to a generally downbeat view of

Australian supply capacity that also emerges in its discussion
of productivity trends. Although its position piece in the August
2025 Statement on Monetary Policy  indicated that lower trend
growth in productivity had no implications for inflation or
monetary policy, more recent statements imply that it does.
More broadly, the minutes for the September meeting revealed
a tendency to contemplate more hawkish interpretations
of data and outcomes. For example, the minutes dismissed

evidence of the labour market easing a bit more than
was forecast by pointing to the changes to the survey
arrangements. At the same time, the minutes contemplated
that the rate of unemployment consistent with inflation
being at target might have risen, even though wages growth
has marginally undershot the RBA’s forecasts and the cost
pressures cited by its business liaison contacts are mostly
non-labour costs. The minutes also included an extended

discussion of the possibility that the global neutral interest
rate might have increased recently. If true, this would imply
that financial conditions were less restrictive. This conclusion
would be hard to square with yield curves that have flattened
since the beginning of the year and other central bank
estimates of the term premium having increased, both of which
suggest tighter financial conditions.
That said, the RBA will find it hard to look past inflation

outcomes in the second half of year that is likely to remain
sticky. We anticipate trimmed mean inflation to record
quarterly outcomes of around 0.9%qtr in Q3 and Q4, remaining
around the current 3½%yr pulse. The Monetary Policy Board
will want to see clear evidence of inflation coming down before
withdrawing policy restrictiveness, so we continue to pencil in
August as the likely date for start of the rate-cutting phase.
